## Thermodynamics & Transport second-comparator crosswalk, closed-system first law (2026-10-28)

Thermodynamics & Transport is the eighth package compared with a second comparator. A new [scope crosswalk](../content/courses/transport/scope-crosswalk.md) sets the 36 lecture titles of MIT OCW 20.110J Fall 2005 beside Open Yale Courses PHYS 200, whose Lecture 20–24 overviews and chapter titles were read. The gap both comparators share and the package lacked was the closed-system foundation: state and path functions, the first law for a fixed amount of matter, heat capacities and the ideal gas, and the adiabatic, isothermal, isobaric and isochoric processes (20.110J Lectures 1–4; Yale Lectures 21–23). Whole-word searches of every file in the course found none of "state function", "internal energy", "ideal gas", "Carnot" or "heat engine". Version 0.7.0 adds an original week-1 reading that supplies that foundation and ends by comparing a reversible with an irreversible expansion and connecting the lost work to the entropy generated, with three objectives, 13 practice items and four cards. A first draft of the crosswalk table, written before the chapter lists and the local readings were rechecked line by line, mislabelled several rows; it was rebuilt from the lecture titles, the chapter lists and whole-word searches. The corrections: thermochemistry and single-component phase equilibria are absent (the lessons use stream enthalpy only), osmotic pressure is taught in lesson 18 but not the other colligative properties, the specific heat of liquids already appeared in lessons 4, 11 and 16, and the mixing free energy with an interaction parameter in lesson 17 is the regular-solution form although the lesson does not name it. The item set was also changed before commit: two planned items repeated numbers the reading already works (263.9 kPa and 83.1 J) and no item computed the entropy generation that the third objective names, so the set now uses fresh scenarios and includes an entropy-generation item. Heat engines and the Carnot cycle (Yale only), thermochemistry and the Joule–Thomson experiment (MIT only), statistical thermodynamics, single-component phase equilibria, solvation and the Debye length remain recorded gaps. The limitation that quoted "five to eleven" items per original lesson was already inexact (lessons 17–22 have two to four) and now reads two to thirteen. The comparison was compiled with AI assistance and has not been reviewed by the owner or an instructor. The package remains partial, unreviewed and formative-only.

## General Chemistry I second-comparator crosswalk, matter waves and octet exceptions (2026-10-28)

General Chemistry I is the ninth package compared with a second comparator. A new [scope crosswalk](../content/courses/general-chemistry-1/scope-crosswalk.md) sets the 35 lectures of MIT OCW 5.111SC Fall 2014 beside UC Irvine Chem 1A, 1B and 1C, a three-quarter sequence by three instructors, using lecture titles, the descriptions of MIT Lectures 3, 4 and 11, and the Chem 1A topic index, which lists topics lecture by lecture. Two strands both comparators teach were absent: the photoelectric effect, de Broglie wavelength, uncertainty principle and particle in a box (MIT Lectures 3–4; Chem 1A Lectures 3–4), and the exceptions to the octet rule with the shapes of steric numbers 5 and 6 (MIT Lecture 11; Chem 1A Lectures 8–10). Version 0.7.0 adds an original week-2 reading and an original week-4 reading, with six objectives, 26 practice items and eight cards. Scoping caught three things: UC Irvine's three courses do not map one-to-one onto the local pair, because Chem 1C and parts of Chem 1B cover acids and bases, electrochemistry and kinetics that the local sequence places in General Chemistry II; MIT's own pages disagree about Lecture 11 (the video list says "Breakdown of the Octet Rule" while the resource slug says "formal charge and resonance"), so its lecture description was read before the claim was made; and the earlier sequence crosswalk had already recorded the wave and particle treatment as a gap and the hypervalent d-orbital hybrid pictures as deliberately excluded, so the octet reading counts electrons and predicts shapes without attributing expanded shells to d orbitals. The handoff still named version 0.5.0 and was corrected.
